Proposed by the user 2026-08-10: a standing pseudo-task to clock
against for grooming, capturing, "what's next" review, and similar
activity that is real work but belongs to no single tracked task.
Captured as TODO on a recommendation, not as a settled decision.

*The concrete argument for it, from this session's own data.* Of the
first ten events in this session's queue, *eight* were =pause=/=resume=
guideposts that reduced to nothing at review time. Guideposts carry no
heading; they inherit whichever one the surrounding
=clock_in=/=clock_out= last named, so guideposts recorded while no clock
is open are unattributable and silently dropped. Meta-work is precisely
when no clock is open. So today that time is not merely misattributed --
it is invisible, which is in direct tension with this project's second
stated goal of trustworthy tracking of where attention actually went.

A standing task, clocked during that work, gives those guideposts a
bracket to attach to. Nothing else has to change for it to work.

*Check org's own mechanism first, per
[[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]].* Org already has
a name for this concept: =org-clock-default-task=, set with
=org-clock-mark-default-task=, a designated fallback task you can clock
into directly. This should almost certainly use that rather than invent
a parallel convention -- exactly the reflex c084553c exists to enforce.

*Two real risks, neither disqualifying:*
- *The dumping-ground failure.* If ambiguous time drifts here by
  default, the record gets *less* informative, not more -- a large
  bucket labelled "misc" answers no question worth asking. Scope it to
  genuinely cross-cutting work, and treat the review pass as the place
  to re-attribute a span that turned out to be about one task.
- *It never reaches DONE.* A permanent open task sits in a sibling group
  alongside the single-NEXT-per-level triggers
  (=--trigger-auto-promote-sole-todo=,
  =--trigger-demote-conflicting-next=). Check how those behave with a
  sibling that is deliberately never finished before relying on it. It
  also never archives, so its =:LOGBOOK:= grows without bound -- probably
  fine for a pure time record, but it is a decision, not an oversight.

Independent of [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] and
useful either way: attribution is resolved from queued
=clock_in=/=clock_out= events both before and after the cutover, so the
bracket does the same job in both worlds.


*Refined 2026-08-10, and it narrows what this heading is for.* The user
proposed the general principle: grooming, defining, amending and
adjusting a future task is *activity on that task*, and should be clocked
against it — leaving its TODO state untouched. Recorded here because it
answers most of what a catch-all was being considered for.

*This was never stated anywhere, though nothing forbade it.* CLAUDE.md's
transition rules say a transition *to* =DOING= opens a clock and one
*from* =DOING= closes it. They never say the converse — that a running
clock implies =DOING= — so clocking a =TODO= heading has always been
legal by the letter and simply undescribed. Confirmed safe in practice
the same day: =org-clock-in-switch-to-state= is nil here, so clocking in
does not touch the keyword, and this heading's own editorial session was
clocked against =02aaae22= while it stayed literally =TODO=.

*Why it is better than a bucket, where it applies.* Attribution is exact:
time spent working out what a task should be lands on that task, next to
the reasoning it produced, and shows up in any report scoped to it.
A catch-all would answer "how much meta-work happened" but never "which
task was I circling."

*So the two are complementary, and this heading shrinks to the residue:*
work that genuinely belongs to no single task — "what's next?" surveys
across the whole backlog, cross-cutting review, triage before a task
exists to attach to. That residue is real but much smaller than the
original framing assumed, which also blunts the dumping-ground risk noted
above: with per-task grooming as the default, anything reaching the
catch-all is there because it truly has no home.

*Consequence worth accepting deliberately:* a =TODO= heading can now
accrue real clock time without ever having been =DOING=. That is correct
— deciding what a task is *is* work — but it means "time spent" and
"was worked on" stop being the same question, and any future reporting
needs to keep them apart.

*Needs a rule in CLAUDE.md* under "State transition rules", stating that
a clock may run on a heading in any state, and that grooming attaches to
the task being groomed. Distinct from
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]], which is scoped to
documenting the post-cutover architecture; this is a general convention
that holds regardless of the refactor's fate.


*Alternative captured 2026-08-11, and it narrows this heading further:*
[[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]] proposes that the review buffer
surface unattributed guideposts and let the human assign them to a
heading retrospectively, rather than requiring anyone to clock into a
standing task in advance. If that lands, this heading is no longer a
mechanism to remember — it is just a convenient assignment target for
spans that genuinely belong to no task.

*Decided 2026-08-21, in conversation with the user, and now scoped into five children.* The standing pseudo-task above becomes a *datetree*: one node per day under a =Review and planning= category, clocked against directly rather than captured beneath.

*The daily node answers this heading's own recorded risk.* It warned that a permanent open task "never archives, so its =:LOGBOOK:= grows without bound -- probably fine for a pure time record, but it is a decision, not an oversight." A day node is bounded by construction and the year rolls into DONE.org on the existing =:ARCHIVE:= route, so the unbounded case never arises.

*The dumping-ground risk is unchanged and gets one concrete guard:* the day node carries *no TODO keyword*, so =claude-code-ide-org--review-suggest-heading= will never propose it -- and after [[id:62c6b1be-4e3c-4ea1-9f0c-19ee70be0e1e][62c6b1be]] it would refuse a container anyway. Every span that lands there costs a deliberate =a= at review. That keeps this a place time is *assigned* to, not a place it *falls*.

*=org-clock-default-task= is adopted*, closing the check-org-first item this heading raised: org already had a name for the concept and it is now wired rather than merely noted. Its limit is worth stating plainly -- it is a live-Emacs convenience for the human, and Claude cannot use it at all, since every clock call here goes through =org_clock_in= with an =:ID:=.

*Creation is on demand, not scheduled.* The user asked whether a cron job or a recurrence should create the node; measured against three consequences -- empty nodes on non-working days, a stored id that can go stale, and a scheduler failure that announces nothing -- the answer was neither. See [[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]].

*The repeater is kept, for the ritual and not the mechanism.* A daily =SCHEDULED: <... +1d>= prompt to actually do review and planning, which is what the original scrum-ish framing was reaching for. It reminds; it does not create.

*One draft was falsified along the way and the finding is in [[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]]:* the outline path in =file+olp+datetree= does not nest the tree. Nesting comes from a =:DATE_TREE:= property. Worth knowing before anyone writes the capture template from the template name alone, which is exactly how the first draft went wrong.

*Correction [2026-08-27 Thu]: "=org-clock-default-task= is adopted … now wired rather than merely noted" is wrong, and contradicts its own next sentence.*

That paragraph says in one breath that org's variable is wired, and then that "Claude cannot use it at all, since every clock call here goes through =org_clock_in= with an =:ID:=". Both cannot be true of a mechanism that is doing the work. [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]] states the fact plainly: *zero references to =clock-default-task= across =modules/= and the Doom config.* It has never been wired.

*What actually shipped is ours, not org's.* Meta-work time is allocated by =claude-code-ide-org--ensure-day-node= plus =org_clock_in= accepting the *category title* in place of an =:ID:=, resolving today's day node on demand and filing the interval against it. That is why the allocation works today and has for days -- the user noticed exactly this and asked.

*So the check-org-first item this heading raised was closed against the wrong evidence.* Org did have a name for the concept, which is what the item asked. It does not follow that we used it, and the body recorded the first as though it settled the second. Being right about the search and wrong about the outcome is the more dangerous shape, because it reads as diligence.

*What =org-clock-default-task= would still buy, narrowly.* One thing: the *human*, in Emacs, clocking meta-work with =C-u C-u C-c C-x C-i= without navigating to today's node first. Nothing for Claude, nothing for the queue. [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]] remains open on those terms and should be judged on them rather than on "org has a name for it" -- a reason this session has now watched fail eight times in two days.

*And the interval it would produce is inactive, necessarily.* Measured 2026-08-27: =org-clock-in= writes =CLOCK: [ts]--[ts]= and org has no active variant of a CLOCK line at all. The active/inactive distinction this project cares about lives on the *annotation* line beneath, never on the CLOCK line -- so what marks such an interval as the human's own is the *absence* of an annotation, which is what [[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]] and =:ID:= b8e6007a (DONE.org) already say.


** TODO Teach org_capture to target a datetree, with an addressable day node :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  datetree target and addressable day node
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:27] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
- State "TODO"       from "NEXT"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:47] \\
  Auto-demoted: superseded by sibling [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][bin/lint-org will drown in datetree scaffolding it can never satisfy]] becoming NEXT.
:END:

=claude-code-ide-org--capture-target-spec= (config.el:1747) resolves exactly three shapes: =(file F)=, =(id ID)= and =(file+olp F CATEGORY)=. A datetree needs a fourth.

*The olp in =file+olp+datetree= does not nest the tree, which is the opposite of what the template's own name suggests.* Read from org-capture.el rather than assumed: the branch resolves the outline path to a marker, goes to it -- and then calls =(widen)= and hands =org-datetree-find-date-create= the date with *no* =keep-restriction= argument. The marker is discarded. Nesting comes from somewhere else entirely.

*Two mechanisms actually control placement*, both in =org-datetree--find-create-group=:

- =keep-restriction= = ='subtree-at-point=, which narrows to the heading at point and sets =org-datetree-base-level= from it. Capture never passes this.
- a =:DATE_TREE:= property, found anywhere in the file by =org-find-property= after the widen. When present, org narrows to that heading and sets the base level from it. *This is the one capture actually uses.*

*Materialized both ways rather than reasoned about.* Without the property, two captures against =* Review and planning= produced two separate =* 2026= trees at *level 1*, outside the category and duplicated. With =:DATE_TREE: t= on the category, one tree nested correctly:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
,* Review and planning
:PROPERTIES:
:DATE_TREE: t
:ARCHIVE:  DONE.org::* Review and planning
:END:
,** 2026
,*** 2026-08 August
,**** 2026-08-19 Wednesday
,**** 2026-08-21 Friday
#+END_EXAMPLE

*=:DATE_TREE:= is permitted on a category by the narrowed rule, not in spite of it.* =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= allows "a *structural* property, which says something about the grouping rather than about work", and names =:ARCHIVE:= as the case that forced the wording. =:DATE_TREE:= is the same kind of statement -- it says how this grouping is organised -- and sits beside =:ARCHIVE:= on identical footing.

*The day node is bare, and that is the part that blocks everything else.* Org writes =**** 2026-08-21 Friday= with no property drawer at all. The whole point of this design is to *clock against the day node*, and every tool here addresses headings by =:ID:=, so the capture path must stamp =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= on the day node at creation. Without that the node exists and is unreachable.

*Verified on org 9.6.15* (the batch Emacs =bin/test= runs). The live Emacs is on *9.8.7*; both carry =org-datetree--find-create-group= and the =:DATE_TREE:= lookup, but the split is [[id:1ed7b2b4-a809-4547-b04f-099a3111fc1e][1ed7b2b4]]'s undeclared-dependency gap showing up in practice, and a datetree test will run against the older one.


** MAYBE Point org-clock-default-task at the day node, and re-point it daily :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  wire and re-point the default task
- State "MAYBE"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-28 Fri 07:55] \\
  buys only human convenience now
:END:

=org-clock-default-task= is org's own answer to "somewhere to clock cross-cutting time", which is why [[id:a1d63f52-4c07-4b9e-8f31-6d2b90c4e7a5][a1d63f52]] pointed at it under c084553c's check-org-first rule. Chosen by the user 2026-08-21, alongside the datetree.

*It is entirely unwired today.* Zero references to =clock-default-task= across =modules/= and =~/.config/doom/config.el=. Nothing to migrate; this is a first wiring.

*It moves every day, and nothing moves it.* The variable holds a *marker*, set from point by =org-clock-mark-default-task=. With a datetree the intended target is a different heading each morning, so on any day after the first the default task points at yesterday. Something has to re-point it -- a =SessionStart= hook, an idle timer, or on-demand resolution when the day node is created. Deciding which is the substance of this heading; the wiring itself is one =setq=.

*It serves the human and not Claude, and that asymmetry should be accepted deliberately rather than discovered.* =org-clock-default-task= is a live-Emacs convenience: clock into it with a keystroke. Claude cannot use it at all -- every clock call here goes through =org_clock_in= with an =:ID:=, and the queue architecture means Claude never touches a live clock in the first place. So this buys *you* one-keystroke meta-work clocking, while Claude still needs the day node resolved to a UUID.

*Do not solve Claude's half here.* Resolving "today's node" to an =:ID:= without pasting a UUID is [[id:915378ac-3bd4-461d-b0f0-83f67fbb3dac][915378ac]]'s subject, under [[id:478d6ec9-1b99-4c6c-88e9-c793ad4b2f22][478d6ec9]]. Building a second lookup here would be the parallel-convention mistake c084553c exists to prevent, one level down.


** DONE bin/lint-org will drown in datetree scaffolding it can never satisfy :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  exempt datetree scaffolding from CREATED
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:32] \\
  must land before the tree exists
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 11:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 12:00] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-21 Fri 11:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 12:00] measure the real lint scope
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16] \\
  revise the level rule for the datetree
- [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] teach the lint about datetree scaffolding
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] \\
  level rule scoped, four mutations verified
:END:
:PLAN:

=bin/lint-org= warns on any heading missing =:CREATED:=. Datetree scaffolding can never satisfy it: org writes =** 2026=, =*** 2026-08 August= and =**** 2026-08-21 Friday= bare, and a year node has no creation moment worth stamping anyway.

*The arithmetic is what makes this a blocker rather than a nuisance.* One day node per working day plus a month node each month is on the order of 300-400 headings a year, against *18 warnings in the whole repo today*. The scaffolding would outnumber the real findings roughly twenty to one inside the first year, and a warning list nobody reads is the same as no lint at all -- the failure mode [[id:e0904e93-b99d-44ad-b641-e3e13aced8c4][e0904e93]] just finished fixing in the session-start report, arriving in a second place.

*Fix it before the tree exists, not after.* Once several hundred warnings are already there the temptation is to silence the check rather than exempt the class, and those are very different decisions.

*Exempt the class, not the file.* The day node is the one that will carry =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= (see [[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]]), so it should stay linted like any other tracked heading; only the *year and month* nodes are pure structure. That distinction is mechanical -- a datetree year or month node is a heading whose title matches org's own datetree format and which sits under a =:DATE_TREE:= heading -- so the exemption can be stated precisely rather than as "skip anything that looks like a date".

*Note this is the same shape as the categories exemption already in the linter*, which skips =:ID:=/=:CREATED:= on top-level headings because they are structure rather than work. Worth reading that code first; this may be one more case for an existing predicate rather than a new branch.

*Measured 2026-08-21 by linting the exact proposed structure, and the scope above is understated.* The warning flood is the least of it — the real blockers are three *errors*, which the pre-commit hook refuses on:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
error: heading has no :ID:: 2026
error: heading has no :ID:: 2026-08 August
error: level-4 heading; the file has three levels (category, task, epic-child): 2026-08-21 Friday
warn : heading has no :CREATED:: 2026
warn : heading has no :CREATED:: 2026-08 August
#+END_EXAMPLE

Two corrections follow. The year and month nodes fail on *=:ID:=*, an error, before they ever reach the =:CREATED:= warning this heading was written about. And the *level-4 rule rejects the day node itself* — the one heading that does carry =:ID:= and =:CREATED:=, and the one we most want linted. Exempting scaffolding does not reach it.

*The level rule is an assertion with a source*, recorded on [[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]] and restated in =bin/lint-org='s header as "the file has three levels (category, task, epic-child)". A datetree makes that false: category, year, month, day is four before any task exists. So this is not a lint tweak but a revision of a documented structural claim about TODO.org, and 3bd3402b should be read before the rule is touched.

*Consequence for sequencing:* proceeding as previously scoped would exempt the two warnings, then hit three errors at [[id:cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2][cd1e974e]] with the category half-written.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-21, before the tree exists.* All three errors are gone and none of them was silenced. =claude-code-ide-org--lint-file= now tracks the innermost enclosing =:DATE_TREE:= heading's level while it maps entries -- =org-map-entries= walks in document order, so an anchor is always seen before its descendants -- and classifies each descendant by a new =claude-code-ide-org--datetree-node-role=.

*The level rule is scoped, not dropped.* 3bd3402b's claim -- 624 headings, zero at level 4 -- was true of a file with no datetree in it. It now reads: three levels outside a =:DATE_TREE:= subtree, and inside one exactly org's year/month scaffolding plus its day node. A level-4 heading anywhere else is still an error, and so is anything *below* a day node, since nothing is filed under one. =bin/lint-org='s header carries the same revised wording; =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= never stated the rule, so nothing there needed changing.

*Gated on org's literal title shapes, not on depth*, and that is the load-bearing choice. A =:DATE_TREE:= category holds real tasks beside its tree -- [[id:cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2][cd1e974e]]'s ritual repeater is a level-2 heading under that very category, exactly where the year node sits -- so a depth-only exemption would have waived =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= for it and for anything ever refiled there. That is the same over-application shape guarded twice in [[id:42808717-2736-4bf4-872e-887f990203ca][42808717]] and [[id:c8a6c5d2-8777-44d2-9900-e56eb03aa2d9][c8a6c5d2]] earlier the same day: a predicate reading position without asking what kind of heading it is looking at. The patterns are org's own =comparefun= regexes, read out of the straight checkout's =org-datetree.el= rather than recalled. Quarter and ISO-week groupings are deliberately not accepted.

*The day node stays linted, which took an explicit fall-through.* Writing the level clause as a branch that matched the day node and did nothing would have exempted the one heading here that most needs =:ID:= -- it is what this project clocks against. The condition is =and=-ed instead, so a day node falls through to the ordinary =:ID:=/=:CREATED:= checks.

*Verified by mutation, four ways, each failing exactly its own test:* removing the year/month exemption, refusing the day node its level-4 allowance, making the role depth-only, and treating the day node as scaffolding. The third is the one that matters -- it is the only mutation the depth-only design would have shipped. Fixtures are literal text and never call =org-datetree-find-date-create=, keeping [[id:1ed7b2b4-a809-4547-b04f-099a3111fc1e][1ed7b2b4]]'s 9.6-vs-9.8 org split out of a lint that only reads structure. =bin/lint-org= on the real files: 0 errors, 23 pre-existing warnings.

*A second datetree is already visible, and this rule does not cover it.* Measured 2026-08-21, hours after shipping: if DONE.org ever takes an =org-archive-location= of the =datetree/= form, org builds a year/month/day tree there *with no =:DATE_TREE:= property on it* and files entries at *level 4* beneath the day node. Against the rule as written that is four errors per archived item -- three scaffolding nodes with no =:ID:=, plus a level-4 heading that is not a day node.

Not a defect in what shipped: the rule was scoped to the structure that existed, and it correctly refuses a shape nobody has adopted. But it means the exemption is keyed on a property org's own archive path never writes, so "recognise a datetree" and "recognise our =:DATE_TREE:= anchor" are not the same predicate. Whoever takes up [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]] or the archive-cadence question should read this rule before choosing an archive location, not after.


** DONE Resolve today's node on demand, never storing or scheduling its ID :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 10:10]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 10:11] \\
  build the on-demand resolver
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:22] \\
  nominated: resolver gates both remaining siblings
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27] \\
  build the on-demand day-node resolver
- [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] build the day-node resolver
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] \\
  resolver shipped, dated from the event
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:46]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:48] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-24 Mon 12:46]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:48] make the category assignable at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 13:02]--[2026-08-24 Mon 13:03] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-24 Mon 13:02]--[2026-08-24 Mon 13:03] make the category assignable at review
:END:
:PLAN:

One function answers "which heading is today's meta-work node", and it is called at the moment something needs the answer rather than run ahead of time. Steps: find the =:DATE_TREE:= heading; =org-datetree-find-date-create= for today, which is org's own idempotent find-or-create; stamp =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= if the node was just made; set =org-clock-default-task= to its marker; return the id.

*Two callers, and the asymmetry between them is the design.* =org_clock_in= calls it when the target names the category, and *creates* if absent. The =SessionStart= hook calls it to refresh =org-clock-default-task= and must *never* create -- a session starting is not evidence that meta-work happened.

*Why on demand rather than scheduled, which was the question that produced this heading.* The user asked 2026-08-21 whether creation should be a cron job or a recurrence. Three consequences decided it:

- *A schedule manufactures empty nodes.* One per calendar day whether or not anything happened -- on the order of 365 a year against a working year's worth of real ones. That is [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]]'s scaffolding problem being fed rather than contained.
- *A stored id goes stale; a computed one cannot.* The premise of the original question -- "the default id would have to be updated to match today's node" -- is only true if something remembers yesterday's. Recomputing deletes the failure mode instead of scheduling around it.
- *Nothing announces a scheduler that stopped running.* A missing node is noticed the moment someone clocks; a silent scheduler failure is noticed at review, days later, as absent time.

*The scheduling tools available to Claude cannot do this job anyway.* =CronCreate= and the =schedule= skill run *cloud* agents, which have no route to this machine's Emacs server. Recorded from the tooling description rather than from a test, but the shape is not marginal. A local scheduler would also fire on non-working days, and -- recursively -- a session spawned to fix attribution generates its own guideposts needing attribution.

*Claude never learns the daily UUID, which is the point.* The target is the stable category title =Review and planning=, reusing the precedent =claude-code-ide-org--capture-target-spec= already argues for: top-level categories are few, human-curated, and never move, so the properties that make titles unsafe elsewhere do not hold. That sidesteps [[id:915378ac-3bd4-461d-b0f0-83f67fbb3dac][915378ac]] rather than waiting on it.

*Shared by [[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]] and [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]]*, which each need part of it -- the day node's =:ID:= and the default-task marker respectively. Build it once here rather than twice there.

*Open design question, recorded 2026-08-21 (user-flagged for their decision, found reviewing the next-session prompt).* "Two callers: =org_clock_in= creates" is unsettled, not settled: =org_clock_in= is a queued tool that by architecture changes nothing when called. Either (a) the resolver runs at *queue time* -- an immediate file write triggered by a queued tool, a new hybrid class like =org_amend=, so the day-node =:ID:= is resolved while "today" is still today; or (b) it runs at *apply time* inside the review command -- pure, but an event queued late Monday and applied Tuesday resolves "today" to the wrong day's node. (a) keeps the timestamp honest and (b) keeps the tool pure; they cannot both hold. Decide before building; the next-session prompt no longer implies this is specified.

*The open question above has three answers, not the two it poses.* Recorded 2026-08-21 at the user's request, so the analysis does not have to be rebuilt; the decision itself is still theirs.

*(a) Queue time -- =org_clock_in= creates the node when called.* Resolves "today" while today is still today, and hands Claude the id inside the session. Against: it breaks the invariant that queued tools change nothing, which was bought with a documented run of desync bugs; a background or concurrent session would write to TODO.org unreviewed; and a node is created even for a clock event the human later dismisses, which is precisely the empty-node problem this heading's on-demand design exists to prevent.

*(b) Apply time, dated "today" -- the review command creates it.* Tools stay pure, every write stays human-triggered, and no node is made for dismissed work. Against, and disqualifying on its own: an event queued Monday 23:00 and applied Tuesday files Monday's work under Tuesday. Silent wrong attribution is the failure this whole project exists to prevent.

*(c) Apply time, dated from the event's own timestamp.* Everything (b) has, with (b)'s only real defect removed -- using data the event already carries, since every queued event is stamped when it is written. Against: Claude never learns the day-node id during the session.

*Recommended: (c), because its single cost is one this heading has already accepted.* The paragraph above says so outright -- "Claude never learns the daily UUID, which is the point", the target being the stable category title =Review and planning=. So (c) is not a compromise between the other two. It strictly dominates (b), and beats (a) without surrendering anything the design wanted.

*Two consequences to know before committing.* =org-clock-default-task= degrades gracefully rather than silently: the =SessionStart= hook still resolves today's node read-only and never creates, so before the first apply the default task is simply unset -- [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]]'s territory, already off the critical path. And a late apply becomes *correct* rather than convenient: meta-work clocked Monday and applied Friday creates Monday's node on Friday, dated Monday. That reads odd until it is set beside the archive behaviour measured the same day on [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]] -- =org-archive-subtree= files by =CLOSED=, not by archive time. "File by when it happened, not by when it was recorded" is then one principle holding in two places, rather than a special case invented here.

*DECIDED 2026-08-21 by the user: option (c).* The resolver runs at *apply time* and dates the node from *the event's own timestamp*, never from "today".

This supersedes the "Two callers, and the asymmetry between them is the design" paragraph above, which says "=org_clock_in= calls it when the target names the category, and *creates* if absent". It does not create. =org_clock_in= queues an event as every queued tool does, and creation happens when that event is applied, against the day the event was stamped. The =SessionStart= half of that paragraph is unchanged and still correct: it resolves read-only and never creates.

*What to build, restated under the decision:* find the =:DATE_TREE:= heading; =org-datetree-find-date-create= for *the event's date*; stamp =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= if the node was just made; set =org-clock-default-task=; return the id. The only change from the original steps is the date argument, which is why this is a smaller build than the open question made it look.

/Recorded by appending, which is itself/ [[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]]/'s defect: the decision sits below the superseded paragraph rather than replacing it, so a reader meets the stale version first./

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-24* (=2b9be1c=), built to the decision recorded above: option (c), apply time, dated from the event.

=claude-code-ide-org-resolve-day-node= takes a TIME and an optional CREATE. =org_clock_in= now accepts the category title =Review and planning= as its target and *creates nothing* -- queuing stays a line appended to a file. =--resolve-item-target= turns that target into a real day node at apply, dated from the item's own timestamp.

*Verified live against the real TODO.org, read-only:* the category is recognised; today resolves to =nil= because no node exists and none was made; 2026-08-21 resolves to =758643f2=, the node that actually exists; and =git status= stayed clean throughout, so read-only really is read-only. That last check matters more than it looks -- the =SessionStart= caller depends on it, and a resolver that quietly created would manufacture exactly the one-node-per-calendar-day problem this heading's design exists to prevent.

*The dating is pinned by a test that fails under the rejected design.* Reintroducing option (b) -- =(current-time)= instead of the event's timestamp -- fails =test-apply-resolves-a-category-target= and nothing else. The fixture asks for a node dated 2026-08-17 and asserts both that org wrote =2026-08-17 Monday= and that nothing dated today appeared, so it cannot pass by accident on a day that happens to match.

*=:CREATED:= is stamped from the event as well*, not from now. A node minted on Friday for Monday's work was created, as a record, on Monday; stamping it Friday would put a date in the drawer that contradicts the heading title directly above it.

*Nesting is load-bearing and is asserted.* =org-narrow-to-subtree= plus =find-date-create='s =keep-restriction= keeps the tree inside the category. Without it org writes a second =* 2026= at level 1, which =bin/lint-org= would then flag as an uncategorised level-1 heading -- the failure [[id:95087d8f-e16d-45d3-bdea-9b179dc672b4][95087d8f]] filed after =* *= swallowed 6462 lines.

*Not built here, and deliberately:* the =SessionStart= refresh of =org-clock-default-task=. The read-only half of the resolver is what that needs and it exists; wiring it is [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]], which this heading always said was off the critical path. Until then the default task is simply unset, which is the graceful degradation the decision anticipated.

*Now unblocked:* the meta-work spans sitting unassigned in the queue from 08-17, 08-22 and today can be assigned to the category and will file themselves under the right day. That was the practical reason this went first.


** DONE Institute the Review and planning category, with its DATE_TREE and ritual repeater :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:21]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 10:10]
:BLOCKER:  ids(e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd)
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 10:11] \\
  institute the category once lint is exempt
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] =>  0:01
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] \\
  institute the Review and planning category
- [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] write the category, repeater and archive mirror
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] \\
  category instituted, lint and placement verified
:END:
:PLAN:

The act of putting the design into TODO.org. Deliberately last: the user's instruction 2026-08-21 was that the branch and the real edit belong together, at the moment the structure lands rather than while it is being scoped.

*The whole initial subtree, as org actually writes it* -- materialized, not sketched, and comma-escaped per [[id:8a23d6ec-ca45-454d-b65f-e46ea9204816][8a23d6ec]]:

The shape it created is documented in =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= under "The meta-work datetree" -- moved there 2026-08-24 because it is a standing structural fact rather than this task's history, and because the =:PROPERTIES:=/=:END:= pairs in the worked example made this body unwrappable: =:END:= is org's drawer terminator and closes a =:PLAN:= drawer wherever it appears.

A matching =* Review and planning= must be added to DONE.org in the same edit, or the =:ARCHIVE:= target is matched as a literal string, fails to resolve, and org silently appends a second near-identical category at the end of the file.

*The repeater reminds; it does not create.* Chosen by the user 2026-08-21 alongside the on-demand resolver, and the division is load-bearing: creation is [[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]]'s job and happens when work happens. If the repeater created the node instead, every calendar day would produce one whether or not anything occurred, and the id would be re-coupled to a schedule -- both of the things the resolver exists to avoid. What the repeater buys is the ritual: a standing daily prompt to actually do review and planning.

*Verified that the repeater and the datetree coexist*, which was not obvious -- the repeater sits at =org-datetree-base-level=, the same level org wants for year nodes. Materialized with two dates months apart: org skipped the non-year sibling, inserted =** 2026= after it, and kept month order (August before December). No clash.

*A category is the one place in this repo a repeater is safe.* [[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]] records the trap: a repeater never reaches DONE, so it has no completable parent, and being one made an epic uncompletable. A category carries no keyword and completes nothing, so the trap does not apply.

*Blocked on [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]] and not on the others.* That heading's own instruction is to fix the linter "before the tree exists, not after", because once several hundred warnings are present the temptation is to silence the check rather than exempt the class. The datetree tooling can land in either order; the linter cannot.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-21*, as one edit across both files: the category with its =:DATE_TREE:= and =:ARCHIVE:=, the ritual repeater, today's day node, and the matching bare =* Review and planning= at the end of DONE.org. Placed last among the categories in both files. Written exactly as materialized above, with one addition -- today's day node (=2026-08-21 Friday=, [[id:758643f2-2046-4894-8a25-7ab44e4dad9a][758643f2]]) was hand-created with =:ID:= and =:CREATED:=, so meta-work can be clocked today rather than waiting on [[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]].

*The DONE.org mirror carries no =:DATE_TREE:=, deliberately.* Archiving moves a subtree under the target heading as an ordinary child; nothing runs a datetree capture against DONE.org, so the property would assert a structure that file does not have. An archived day node lints there as an ordinary level-2 heading, since it keeps the =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= it was created with.

*=bin/lint-org= on the real files with the tree present: 0 errors, 23 warnings* -- the same 23 as before it existed. That is [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]]'s fix verified against the real structure rather than against fixtures, which is the check that could only be run at this moment.

*Placement verified against the real file, not a sketch.* A copy was driven through =org-datetree-find-date-create= for two dates months apart. Both landed *inside* the category: =2026-08-22 Saturday= nested under the existing =2026-08 August= in date order, and a new =2026-12 December= after it in month order. The repeater sits at =org-datetree-base-level=, the same level org wants for year nodes, and org skipped it rather than clashing with it.

*One consequence worth knowing before 9575e65b lands.* Org writes day nodes bare, so any node org creates that the resolver has not stamped will fail the lint with =heading has no :ID:=. That is the intended guard, not a gap: a day node without an =:ID:= exists and is unreachable, and the lint now says so loudly instead of the node being silently unusable.

- [2026-08-13 Thu 11:04]--[2026-08-13 Thu 11:08]

- State "TODO"       from "WAIT"       [2026-08-14 Fri 11:56]
:END:

*Promoted out of [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] on 2026-08-13.*

It arose from that epic's work but is not required for it to complete: the queue
can be finished and correct without ever gaining capture/amend events. It also
has its own plan file, its own scope decisions and its own deferred phases — a
child with a plan file of its own is usually an epic wearing a child's clothes.
Left as a level-2 task rather than declared an epic, since it has no children
yet; it will become one naturally if capture and amend split.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/capture-amend-queue.md][Plan]]

*Plan approved 2026-08-13, scoped by two decisions the user made during
planning:* v1 covers *capture and amend* only — the structural writers
(=org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling=, =org_sort_children=)
stay immediate — and capture *writes through when the file is free*,
queueing only when it is busy, so today's immediacy survives and only
contended writes defer. That is the user's original lock idea made
non-blocking, and it supersedes the "always queue" reading of step 3
below.

Note the plan lives in its own file rather than this conversation's
=hazy-moseying-sky.md=: that path is already the permanent link for [[id:6b1e73c4-25da-4f0e-8a51-9c0d3f7ab214][6b1e73c4]] and
[[id:f290161c-f9e7-4bfb-9adf-4c5630d4c5ae][f290161c]], and Claude Code reuses one plan file per conversation, so planning
here overwrote it. Restored from =plans/= and the new plan given its own name,
so both older links still resolve to the plan they were written for.

*Read by the user 2026-08-13* — both the original draft below and the
=create-lockfiles= research appended to step 1. The "study this before
signing off" reminder it was captured under is discharged.

*No verdict was recorded, and none should be inferred.* The user said
only that they had reviewed it and that the heading could be amended
accordingly; they did not approve the recommendation, reject it, or
choose between its three steps. So this remains a proposal Claude
drafted, not an agreed design, and the three load-bearing claims are
still open to challenge: that a *blocking* lock is the wrong primitive,
that the underlying need is a missing queue rather than a missing lock,
and that the ordering below is the right one. Anyone acting on this
should get an explicit decision first.

*The ask, in the user's words:* so many times they want to ask a
question or make a proposal while something else is in flight, which
often leads to something new to capture or amend. The literal proposal
was a file or Emacs buffer lock/semaphore on =TODO.org= and friends,
causing concurrent sessions to wait their turn while another holds it.

*What actually collides today.*

The queue already removed most of the contention: =todo= and clock
events are append-only, per-session, written blind with =O_APPEND=, so
two sessions cannot corrupt each other there by construction. What
remains is *direct writes to the file*:

- =org_capture=, =org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling= and
  =org_sort_children=, which all still =save-buffer= immediately
- the org skill's prose edits via Claude Code's =Edit= tool, which
  bypass Emacs entirely and write to disk
- the user's own hand-edits in the live buffer
- =.git/index=, a separate incident already owned by
  [[id:2aeb65d6-350e-4c75-811e-4a748044fe2c][2aeb65d6]]

Not hypothetical: on 2026-08-13 two edits in one session hit "the file
had been modified on disk since you last read it". The ad-hoc
mitigation in use — checking =buffer-modified-p= before touching the
file — is a hand-rolled version of what this heading proposes.

*Why a blocking lock looks like the wrong primitive.*

Recorded as the argument against, because "why not just take a lock" is
the obvious question a future reader will ask.

1. *Waiting reintroduces the failure this architecture exists to avoid.*
   A session blocked on a lock burns wall-clock with no way to report
   why, and waits forever if the holder crashed. Same shape as the
   non-interactive =emacsclient= hang that pushed this project to
   append-only.
2. *The contention is asymmetric.* The human is the authoritative
   writer; Claude sessions should yield to them and never the reverse. A
   symmetric semaphore models the wrong relationship.
3. *It cannot be enforced where it matters most.* The =Edit= tool
   consults neither Emacs nor any lockfile, so any lock is advisory for
   exactly the writer most likely to be mid-flight. A mechanism that
   looks like mutual exclusion but is not is worse than an honest
   advisory one.
4. *Stale locks are stale clocks again.* An owner, a TTL and a recovery
   path — the subsystem
   [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]] is currently
   deciding whether to retire, rebuilt for a new resource.

*The reframe this rests on — the part most worth challenging.*

The ask may not be mutual exclusion at all. Note where the user's own
sentence ends: the interruption *leads to something new to capture or
amend*. That is a missing queue, not a missing lock — state changes
already work that way; capture and amend do not.

If that reframe is wrong — if the real need is genuinely "stop the
other writer" rather than "do not lose my thought" — then the
recommendation below is wrong too, and a fail-fast lock alone is the
answer.

*Proposed, cheapest first.*

1. *Turn on =create-lockfiles=.* Measured 2026-08-13: it is =nil=, Doom's
   default, so Emacs's own mechanism is currently off entirely. One
   line. It will not stop the =Edit= tool, but it makes Emacs-mediated
   collisions visible rather than silent. Checked before proposing
   anything bespoke, per
   [[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]]'s standing
   instruction.

   *Why Doom disables it, checked 2026-08-13 at the user's request.*
   =lisp/doom-emacs.el:462= sets it under a =Backup & autosave files=
   heading, sharing one comment with =make-backup-files=: "Don't
   generate backups or lockfiles. ... This is better suited to version
   control, and I don't want world-readable copies of potentially
   sensitive material floating around our filesystem." Older Doom
   (=core/core-editor.el=) worded it the same way.

   That rationale is about *backups*, and does not actually describe a
   lock file. Per the Emacs Lisp manual, a lock file is a symlink named
   =.#FILE= whose *target* is the string =user@host.pid:boot=; it holds
   no contents of the edited file at all. So the "copies of sensitive
   material" argument does not apply to the thing it is being used to
   justify — lockfiles were bundled in with backups under one heading
   and inherited a comment written for backups. A residual privacy point
   survives (the target leaks username, host and PID), but that is a
   much smaller claim than the one made.

   The real-world objection in the wider community is *tooling*, not
   privacy: =.#FILE= is a dangling symlink, and file watchers and build
   tools choke on it (e.g. =ember-cli= issue #6527, "=(setq
   create-lockfiles nil)= shouldn't be necessary for Emacs usage"). That
   risk is low for this repo — nothing here watches the tree — but it is
   the reason to check before flipping it globally rather than
   per-directory.

   *And one point actively in favor, which is why this step is listed at
   all:* on detecting a lock, Emacs calls =ask-user-about-lock=, which
   offers grab / ignore / abort. It *asks*; it does not wait. That is
   precisely the fail-fast shape argued for in step 2, already
   implemented upstream.
2. *A fail-fast advisory lock in the MCP write tools* — a refusal, not a
   wait: "another session is writing TODO.org; queued instead." Fast
   failure is debuggable and cannot deadlock.
3. *Extend the queue to capture/amend proposals.* The interjection
   becomes an event, nothing waits, and the existing human review pass
   applies it — which also makes it reviewable rather than immediate,
   the property already chosen deliberately for state changes.

Steps 2 and 3 overlap: if 3 lands, 2 may be unnecessary, since there
would be nothing left that needs to write immediately.


* TODO Bracket queued spans with WAIT transitions, so the heading guess stops latching :code:

:CATEGORY: Queue

:CREATED:  [2026-08-13 Thu 18:34]

:LOGBOOK:

  captured from conversation, awaiting your read

- [2026-08-13 Thu 19:24]--[2026-08-13 Thu 19:25] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 08:21]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:22] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-14 Fri 08:21]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:22] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 08:26]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:28] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-14 Fri 08:26]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:28] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 08:37]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:44] =>  0:07
- [2026-08-14 Fri 08:37]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:44] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 08:49]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:59] =>  0:10
- [2026-08-14 Fri 08:49]--[2026-08-14 Fri 08:59] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 09:02]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:04] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-14 Fri 09:02]--[2026-08-14 Fri 09:04] suggested span, accepted at review
:END:

*Noticed 2026-08-13, in conversation, after the pattern had already been
used twice without anyone naming it.* =WAIT= has quietly acquired a
second, narrower meaning here: the resting state at the end of a Claude
work increment, meaning "the ball is in the human's court". That is not
what the =#+TODO:= table says it means (generic "Blocked or waiting on
someone"), and the difference is load-bearing.

*Why it matters for the queue.* The suggestion machinery's known defect
is that the guess *latches*: measured live 2026-08-13, three spans
covering eight hours all suggested a heading that had long gone quiet.
It latches because a =clock_in= sets a lane's current heading and
*nothing ever unsets it* — there is no event meaning "Claude stopped
working on this."

A =DOING -> WAIT= transition is exactly that event, and it has three
properties a heading guess does not:

1. *A hard right edge.* Guideposts after it provably do not belong to
   that heading. That alone kills the latch.
2. *The =WAIT -> DOING= gap is human time, not attention.* By
   construction that interval is "the human had not answered yet" — it
   is not work on the task, yet today it is clustered into a span and
   offered for assignment like any other.
3. *It is already in the queue.* =todo= events carry state and timestamp
   whether or not anyone has applied them, so a reader can bracket spans
   without the file having moved. No new event kind, no schema change.

Composes with [[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]]:
commits and =:CREATED:= stamps inside a span's window are the evidence,
and =WAIT= edges are the bounds on which windows are worth looking at.
It is also a cheaper, mechanical partial answer to
[[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]], which proposes
asking Claude to narrate the same gaps.

*Two things to settle before this becomes a rule.*

- *The keyword is ambiguous.* A =WAIT= meaning "waiting on a vendor to
  email back" carries none of this. The discriminator is probably not
  the keyword at all but the event's =source=/=session_id=: a =WAIT= a
  *session* queued is a Claude/human handoff, one a human set by hand is
  not. That distinction already exists in every queue line and is
  currently unused.
- *It is only as reliable as its consistency.* A session that ends
  leaving a heading =DOING= has a silently missing bracket, and reading
  absence as "still working" is the wrong default in exactly the case
  that matters. Worth deciding whether that is a lint
  ([[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]]) or a hook.

*Provenance, recorded because it bears on how much weight to put on
this:* the pattern came from the user's own correction, not from design.
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] was set to =WAIT=
at their explicit instruction on 2026-08-13, and the same call was then
applied to [[id:b5f94b88-76e3-4a3c-ac73-729f34d33691][b5f94b88]] by
imitation. Two instances, one person, one afternoon — an observed habit
worth testing, not a measured regularity.

May also bound the damage from
[[id:f4e628ce-e653-429a-8f3c-0f5d4241b139][f4e628ce]] and
[[id:09c134c4-31eb-4f6c-aa10-f836d1d28f16][09c134c4]], both of which are
about guideposts that are missing or phantom: a bracket constrains what
a stray guidepost can be attributed to, even when the guidepost itself
is wrong.

*Correction, 2026-08-14: the claim above that "nothing ever unsets it" is
wrong, and was already wrong when this heading was written.*

=claude-code-ide-org--review-suggest-heading= (=config.el= line 3097 as
of today) already clears the active heading on any =todo= event carrying
=DONE=, =CANCELLED=, =WAIT=, =TODO=, =NEXT= or =MAYBE= — that is, on
every keyword except =DOING= and =PLANNING=. It arrived in =3c6b3f2=
("Implement 3d0487f4: unassigned spans, with a suggested heading") on
2026-08-13, hours *before* this heading was captured, and the
latching-guess comment sits directly above it. Found by grep on
2026-08-14 while inventorying keyword touch points for
[[id:c954f650-b92f-4920-9967-ab6c44577b30][c954f650]]; it was not
verified when this heading was written, which is the actual lesson.

So the mechanism this heading proposes building *exists*, and it is
already keyed the way the design conclusion below recommends — on any
transition out of =DOING=/=PLANNING=, not on =WAIT= specifically. Two
things survive the correction, and they are what is left of the idea:

1. *The gap is data, not logic.* The bracket only fires when a =todo=
   event exists to fire it. A session that ends leaving a heading =DOING=
   emits nothing, so the guess keeps latching — which is exactly the
   "only as reliable as its consistency" caveat recorded above, now
   promoted from a caveat to the whole remaining problem. Whether that is
   answered by a lint
   ([[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]]), a =Stop=-hook
   transition, or an inferred bracket at review time is undecided.
2. *The denylist should be an allowlist.* Testing for "not =DOING= and
   not =PLANNING=" instead of naming six keywords would make it immune to
   any future change to the keyword set — including the =REVIEW=
   proposal now recorded on
   [[id:c954f650-b92f-4920-9967-ab6c44577b30][c954f650]], which would
   otherwise need remembering to add a seventh string here. A keyword
   forgotten in that list fails silently, by latching.

*What this heading is now about,* therefore: not "build a bracket" but
"make sure a bracket event always gets emitted, and make the test
robust to the keyword set changing." The =WAIT= framing in the title is
vestigial — the keyword was never the load-bearing part, and the title
should be retitled if this heading is picked up.


* TODO Type a span's annotation at review time, not by hand-editing it after :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Apply
:ID:       8028e875-3032-4ade-949e-414d9861b928
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 12:27]
:END:

*Observed 2026-08-14, in the file rather than in the abstract.* The user
hand-edited an applied annotation on
[[id:4f9e552a-e1a9-48dd-8801-3f23a31b9e32][4f9e552a]] after the fact,
rewriting =suggested span, accepted at review= to =task definition=. The
commit record confirms the correction was right — =d34c809= landed at
08:58, before the 09:09--09:42 span opened, so that interval really was
task definition and not the implementation it sat next to.

*The mechanism, read rather than assumed*
(=claude-code-ide-org--review-annotation-label=, =config.el= line 3298 as
of today). It returns one of three things:

- the enclosing =clock_in='s note, when the span inherited one — which is
  where a genuinely informative line like =show commits and :CREATED: in
  span windows= comes from;
- the empty string, for a bracketed span whose =clock_in= carried no note;
- =assigned at review= / =suggested span, accepted at review= for an
  *unbracketed* span, reconstructed from loose guideposts.

*The gap is the third case, and it is the case that matters.* An
unbracketed span is unbracketed precisely because no session ever said
what it was working on — so it is the one span with no note to inherit,
and the label it gets instead says only *that* it was reconstructed. Yet
at review the human has just decided what it was; that is what accepting
it means. The information exists, in someone's head, at exactly the
moment the line is written, and is discarded.

*A second finding, and it argues against the naive fix.* The hand-edit
*destroyed the provenance*. That boilerplate is not filler — the function's
docstring makes a deliberate case for it: provenance is "the one thing a
reader cannot recover from the timestamps," namely that the interval was
reconstructed and confirmed rather than clocked as it happened, which
matters for a record whose trustworthiness is a stated project goal.
Overwriting it in place trades one irrecoverable fact for another. They
are not competing, and a typed annotation should *append* to the
provenance rather than replace it — =task definition, accepted at review=
keeps both.

*Open, and worth settling before building:* whether the prompt belongs at
mark time (while the span's evidence lines are still on screen, which is
the argument for it) or at apply; whether it fires for every span or only
unbracketed ones; and whether an empty answer is allowed to fall through
to today's behaviour, which it probably must, since a review pass that
cannot be completed silently is worse than a vague label.

*Not blocking anything, and deliberately small.* It changes what one
function returns and adds one prompt. Related to
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] by adjacency — that
heading put the evidence on screen, and this one would capture the
conclusion the human draws from it — but neither requires the other.

  plan the pipeline epic and its four children

- State "DOING"      from "PLANNING"   [2026-08-18 Tue 08:54]
- [2026-08-18 Tue 08:56]--[2026-08-18 Tue 08:56] plan span pipeline end to end

- [2026-08-18 Tue 09:32]--[2026-08-18 Tue 09:33] plan span pipeline end to end
CLOCK: [2026-08-18 Tue 09:52]--[2026-08-18 Tue 09:54] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-18 Tue 09:52]--[2026-08-18 Tue 09:54] plan span pipeline end to end
CLOCK: [2026-08-18 Tue 10:01]--[2026-08-18 Tue 10:03] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-18 Tue 10:01]--[2026-08-18 Tue 10:03] plan span pipeline end to end
:END:

A span's duration is currently wrong in both directions, and the error
enters at three separate stages. Measured 2026-08-17 across all 834
post-cutover guideposts: kind-blind clustering totals *39.10 h* against
*21.59 h* for =resume= -> =pause= pairing — an overcount of 17.50 h, about
45%, because every idle gap under twenty minutes is absorbed into the
surrounding span. In the other direction, six turns exceeded the
threshold and were rendered as zero-width points, 4.88 h invisible.

*The three stages, which is why this is a parent rather than one fix:*

- *Input* — guideposts that record something that did not happen. A
  background task completing re-invokes the model, fires
  =UserPromptSubmit=, and appends a =resume= with no user present.
- *Transform* — =--aggregate-guideposts= maps =:ts= over events and never
  reads =:kind:=, so it clusters an undifferentiated stream. But
  =pause= -> =resume= means nobody was there and =resume= -> =pause=
  means one turn was running; the threshold was tuned for the first and
  inverts on the second. Separately, =--trigger-auto-clock-in= opens a
  live clock that nothing stops, so two mechanisms write CLOCK lines
  with no knowledge of each other.
- *Output* — a lone timestamp becomes a zero-width item that can only be
  dismissed. That is the transform's failure made visible, not a
  cosmetic annoyance.
- *Record* — 98 post-cutover CLOCK lines across TODO.org and DONE.org
  carry the accumulated error.

*Why now, and not later.* The bias profile inverts under the working mode
this project is moving toward. Long unattended stretches mean fewer turn
boundaries, so the overcount shrinks and the long-turn undercount grows —
the defect becomes dominant exactly when the human is least able to notice
it, and the work least observed becomes the work least accurately
recorded. That is the opposite of what CLAUDE.md's second stated goal
asks for.

*Deliberately not a child:
[[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]].* It carries a
=+1m= repeater and never reaches =DONE=, and its own body records being
promoted out of an epic for exactly that reason — "a recurring
measurement has no terminal state and therefore no completable parent".
Making it a child here would repeat the trap
[[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]] sat in. It is
referenced instead, and its subject changes if this work lands: the
threshold is not the lever once the aggregator reads =:kind:=, so what
that recurring measurement is *for* needs restating.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/elegant-questing-lamport.md][Plan]] — approved 2026-08-18. Covers all five
children; each links this same file rather than carrying one of its
own, following 02aaae22 / c556a9ed.


** DONE Background-task notifications write phantom resume guideposts :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-19 Wed 08:05]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       09c134c4-31eb-4f6c-aa10-f836d1d28f16
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 17:46]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-12 Wed 17:47] \\
  phantom resume inflates human-present time
CLOCK: [2026-08-18 Tue 18:14]--[2026-08-18 Tue 18:16] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-18 Tue 18:14]--[2026-08-18 Tue 18:16] classify unmatched resumes from data on disk
- State "DONE"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-19 Wed 08:05] \\
  re-queued, lost to my git checkout
:END:
:PLAN:

Observed 2026-08-12 during the subagent experiment behind
[[id:0d789b68-faca-4911-868a-fdd70acbfd40][0d789b68]]. A background
task completing re-invokes the model, which fires =UserPromptSubmit=,
which appends a =resume= guidepost — with no user having sent anything:

: 17:22:41  resume  UserPromptSubmit   (agent 1 finished ~17:21:47)
: 17:23:50  resume  UserPromptSubmit   (agent 2 finished ~17:23:15)

The events are byte-identical to a genuine prompt's; no field
distinguishes them. This matters because CLAUDE.md defines the
pause/resume stream as tracking *active work time only* — "paused the
moment Claude Code stops and is waiting on the user, resumed the moment
the user sends the next prompt." A robot wake-up now reads as the human
returning to the keyboard, and since
=--aggregate-guideposts= reconstructs human spans from exactly these
timestamps, the effect is to inflate apparent human-present time in
every =:suggested:= interval that spans a background completion.

Affects any background task, not just subagents. Note the guidepost
carries =agent_id: nil= even when the notification came from an agent —
the hook fires in the parent's turn, not the agent's — so filtering on
=agent_id= is *not* the fix.

Not yet investigated: whether the hook payload distinguishes a real
submission from a task-completion re-invocation at all. If it does not,
the honest options are to accept the inflation and document it, or to
stop treating =resume= as evidence of human presence.

*Promoted out of [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] on
2026-08-14,* alongside
[[id:f4e628ce-e653-429a-8f3c-0f5d4241b139][f4e628ce]], which it pairs
with directly — both are about guideposts that are missing or phantom,
and [[id:d1cf852a-696a-4195-a4a7-039439c1f4b7][d1cf852a]] already notes
that a bracket bounds the damage from either. Filed under the epic by
proximity rather than membership: the queue is complete without it.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/elegant-questing-lamport.md][Plan]] — the epic's plan document, which
carries this child's design rather than a file of its own.

:END:
*Ran the free check 2026-08-18, and it says do not instrument.* The plan
asked for this before writing any code: 891 post-cutover guideposts
across 16 sessions, classified from data already on disk.

| unmatched =resume=                                | 2 |
| ...handoff to a background job                    | 1 |
| ...in flight, the stream still open               | 1 |
| ...unexplained                                    | 0 |
| =resume= -> =resume= adjacencies                  | 9 |
| ...with an agent finishing within 5 minutes       | 0 |
| ...followed by real work (a =pause= 8s-1959s on)  | 9 |

Every one of the nine is an interrupt or a queued follow-up — a second
=UserPromptSubmit= before the first turn's =Stop= fired, with gaps of
30s to 1705s and genuine work after each. That is exactly the hypothesis
the plan proposed testing first, and it holds with nothing left over.
The one handoff is
[[id:9202b39d-49e4-441c-89f2-382feb4ef1ea][9202b39d]], found earlier
today and not a phantom either.

*Not phantom-free evidence, and it must not be read that way.* Only
*two* subagent runs exist post-cutover (2 =clock_in=, 2 =clock_out=,
2 distinct agents). The condition that produced the original observation
barely occurred, so "no phantoms found" is close to uninformative on its
own. The case for closing rests on the next paragraph, not this one.

*The harm has no mechanism left, whether or not phantoms still fire.*
This heading's stated defect is that a robot wake-up "reads as the human
returning to the keyboard" and so "inflates apparent human-present
time". Nothing claims human-present time any more.
[[id:b8e6007a-ae13-4b13-bb2e-a5df39435af0][b8e6007a]] made every
queue-derived annotation *inactive*, on the finding that the queue
records agent activity and nothing else, and that conflating the two
makes the agenda unreadable. And since
[[id:226ed53b-c302-4529-870f-a42962b50bde][226ed53b]]'s step 2(b) the
only thing written as duration is a =resume= -> =pause= run. A phantom
=resume= marks the moment the model was genuinely re-invoked and began
working; the run that follows it is real agent time, and counting it is
*correct* under the semantics the record now has.

So the defect dissolved sideways — not by anything done to it, but
because the quantity it corrupted stopped being claimed. Worth naming as
the same shape as
[[id:31f766ab-90ed-4950-bc5b-787528c8fe84][31f766ab]]'s cause 2, which
the plan *predicted* would dissolve and did not: predictions about which
defects evaporate under a refactor have now been wrong once and right
once, so neither should be trusted without the check.

*The one residual is a sentence, not a mechanism.* CLAUDE.md still says
a clock is "resumed the moment the user sends the next prompt", which is
precisely what a background wake-up falsifies. That line is already
owned by [[id:d39f5f0d-bee3-4aa4-8494-0bddd07e9067][d39f5f0d]], so it is
left there rather than duplicated here.

*Nothing was built, deliberately.* The plan's fallback — record
=transcript_path= plus an offset in =bin/hooks/queue-append= — is not
warranted: it would answer a question whose answer no longer changes any
number.
